# Weakenings of the suite

Every change that made the suite weaker, and why. The suite guard (`tests/guard`) reads this file: a weakening it finds passes only when the same change adds its row here, with an anchor that exists and an approver its configuration allows for that kind. Rows are only ever added; the guard refuses a change that edits or removes one.

Kinds and who approves them by default: `test-removed`, `expected-changed`, `docs-example-changed`, `sabotage-changed`, `sabotage-removed`, `harness-changed`, `law-changed`, `finding-weakened` and `ratchet-grew` need a fresh reviewer agent (`reviewer <agent>`); `promise-changed`, `guard-changed`, `ci-changed` and `threshold-lowered` need the owner (`owner`). An anchor is `row <n>`, `I-<id>`, `S<report>-<n>`, `ruling <n>` of `spec/questions.md`, `Runes <version>`, or a path the change touches.

| Date | What | Kind | Reason | Anchor | Approved by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-11-16 | tests/acceptance/waiver-key.test.ts and tests/acceptance/hostile.test.ts: the expected outputs of the waiver tests gain the law-change line | expected-changed | Commit dd28f37 makes check name a waiver only where the type reads waivers; the output of a change that edits the law now carries the line `note: this change edits the law that judges it: ...` of ruling 78, which commit bce54b8 sets in spec/commands-slice.md section 8 and rows 133 and 300 to 304, and the expected text follows that line; the final reviewer examined the change | ruling 78 | reviewer final reviewer of group 2, 2026-11-16 |
| 2026-11-16 | S5-6: explain of a path with a segment over 255 bytes says it does not exist yet, never internal | test-removed | Commit a2deb2c renames and rewrites the hostile test of S5-6: it expects usage and exit 2 as test 245 says, and adds a segment written with escapes that is measured by its bytes; question 135 holds the choice, and the final reviewer examined it | ruling 135 | reviewer final reviewer of group 2, 2026-11-16 |

| 2026-11-16 | tests/harness/{merge.ts,shards.ts,sets.ts,shards.json} | harness-changed | merge.ts and shards.ts compare the path they were started by with the real path of their module, as the six entry points of the guard, the gate and the sabotage run already do, so started through a linked folder, as macOS keeps /var, they judge instead of exiting 0 in silence; sets.ts gains the PLATFORM_TESTS entry of the test that starts all eight entry points through a linked folder and no entry, system or behaviour is removed or changed; shards.json is planned again by `node tests/harness/shards.ts plan` for the one new test file and drops no file; the fresh reviewer of 2026-11-16 asked for exactly this change | ruling 95 | reviewer fresh reviewer of 2026-11-16 |

| 2026-11-17 | .github/workflows/release.yml | ci-changed | The push of the tag v0.1.0-alpha.1 ended the release run 44026288012 in startup_failure: ci.yml's merge jobs ask for actions: read to read the start of their run, and a called workflow cannot hold a permission its caller withholds, while release.yml granted only contents: read; the change grants the job verify, which calls ci.yml, actions: read beside contents: read, reaches only the called CI and leaves the pack and publish jobs as they were. Krzysztof, 2026-11-17 (translated from Polish), picking option A (fix it and move the tag; the label is not his wording) | .github/workflows/release.yml; approves bf40330ae03df62a | owner |
| 2026-11-17 | tests/properties/support.ts: the draw of every property is steered toward the class that would first fall below its share | harness-changed | The reach of a property was checked against a random draw, so a seed could leave a class below its claimed share although the generator reaches it (R-111, seed 819656490, 15 of 153 cases against 0.1, for a class the generator draws about one time in eight); the draw now draws again, up to 1,000 times, until the case lies in the class due first, so the cases that run hold every claimed share of this suite's claims, and no share or class changed. Given up: the reach no longer fails when a generator's own rate of a class falls below the share it claims, as long as 1,000 draws in a row still find the class; it still fails for a class the generator cannot reach, and for one so rare that 1,000 draws in a row miss it | tests/properties/support.ts | reviewer fresh reviewer of the release fixes, 2026-11-17 |
| 2026-11-17 | .github/workflows/release.yml | ci-changed | The release run no longer runs nightly.yml again: the job nightly reads through the API whether the tagged commit, the github.sha that verify and pack build, has a run of nightly.yml from a schedule or a dispatch that ended in success, and refuses to publish with an error that names what is missing when it has none, or when the tag no longer names that commit; verify keeps calling ci.yml with contents: read and actions: read, pack needs both jobs, and the publish job is as it was. Krzysztof, 2026-11-17 (translated from Polish), picking option A (require a green Nightly on the same commit; the label is not his wording) | .github/workflows/release.yml; approves fe09b1ab976e4f03 | owner |

| 2026-11-18 | .github/workflows/ci.yml | ci-changed | ci.yml drops the cache of .grammars, the Grammars step and the upload of the grammar-cache artifact from the build job, and the download of that artifact from every job that used it. Runes 1.2.0 ships the 13 grammars Lawcheck loads, built and checked against their sha256, and npm run build copies them from the installed package with no network, so no .grammars folder is made and the upload, which fails when it finds no files, would fail the run. Every job still runs npm ci and builds dist/ or downloads it from the build job as before, and no test, matrix, shard or trigger changes, so the same tests run against grammars with the same bytes as in alpha.1. The other pages link a code as `codes.md#<code>`, so a reader lands on its heading. | 2026-11-22 | .github/workflows/release.yml | ci-changed | release.yml gains the job latest, which runs after the publish job for a version published under the dist-tag alpha: it installs npm 11.21.2, reads from the registry that alpha names that version, adds the dist-tag latest to it with npm dist-tag over OIDC, and reads both tags back, and it fails unless alpha and latest name the new version; a re-run after a newer alpha fails at the first read and leaves latest where it is. One publish sets one dist-tag and npm refuses a version it already holds, so a second publish cannot add latest. The job holds contents: read and id-token: write, runs no code of the repository and no install script, and needs "Allow npm dist-tag" turned on for the trusted publisher of release.yml on npmjs.com; the header comment names it beside the publish job. The jobs verify, nightly, pack, publish and GitHub release run as before, the GitHub release does not wait for the new job, and no test changes. A red latest job makes the Release run red, and docs.yml deploys the site only after a green Release run, so while that setting is off the site does not deploy until the setting is on and a re-run of the job latest ends green. Re-run only the failed jobs of such a run: a re-run of all jobs finds the version on npm, skips the publish, the job latest and the GitHub release, and ends green with latest where it was. | 2026-11-23 | .github/workflows/release.yml | ci-changed | The publish job publishes an alpha under the dist-tag latest, and the job alpha then adds the dist-tag alpha to that version. Both reads of the dist-tags repeat every 10 seconds, for up to 5 minutes, while they name an older version, and a newer version fails at once. The publish job and the job alpha change, and so does a comment in the pack job; no other job, step, trigger, matrix or permission changes, and npm and the OIDC permission stay as they are. Known: the registry's top-level description and homepage are still those of the 0.0.0 stub, since the alpha versions published so far went under alpha and moving latest with a dist-tag did not change them. Not known: whether a publish under latest sets them, which is expected and not verified on this package before the next release. npm 11 sends a top-level description, but no homepage and no top-level readme, so the README on the npm page is not fixed by this change. What is given up: latest moves at the publish, before the checks, so a failed job alpha leaves latest on the new alpha; and a first publish of a version older than the newest alpha moves latest back. | 2026-11-24 | .github/workflows/release.yml | ci-changed | The job alpha orders versions as semver does, so a stable 0.1.0 on latest fails a re-run of an older alpha at once and no longer after 5 minutes, since sort -V ranked 0.1.0-alpha.N above 0.1.0; a read the registry fails to answer, as with a 5xx, is read again like a stale one, and neither loop sleeps after its last read; the last error says which version a dist-tag names. The job alpha also runs when the publish was skipped because npm already holds the version, so a re-run of all jobs after a good publish moves alpha and no longer ends green with alpha where it was; the advice of row 589 to re-run only the failed jobs is no longer needed. The workflow gains the concurrency group release with no cancelling of a run in progress, so two tags never move the dist-tags together; GitHub replaces a waiting run when a third tag is pushed behind it. The comment above the job alpha says that a re-run of the job repeats only the move of alpha, and the comments in the job alpha say how the reads repeat. Kept as the row of 2026-11-23 says: a first publish of an alpha older than the newest alpha moves latest back, since the order of the tags is the owner's. Two earlier rows left out comments they changed: the row of 2026-11-23 for release.yml left out the header sentence on Allow npm dist-tag at line 4, the comment above the publish step and the comment above the job alpha, and the row of 2026-11-23 for nightly.yml left out the rewritten sentence at its head that says where the network is open (environments, repeat, deadlines, docs and corpus) and where it is closed. No other job, step, trigger or permission changes. What is given up: a re-run of all jobs of an old alpha, after latest has moved on, now ends red where it ended green | .github/workflows/release.yml; approves 9c9474ea1c4693c8 | owner |
